### [SUBPART 5]{.HLabel2}[Gases Under Pressure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819155 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819156 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[7.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.5 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Subpart.

[[critical temperature]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[critical temperature]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the temperature
    above which a pure gas cannot be liquefied, regardless of the degree
    of compression. ([température critique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[gas under pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[gas under pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a product that
    consists of a gas contained in a receptacle at a gauge pressure of
    200 kPa or more at 20°C, or that is liquefied, or liquefied and
    refrigerated, but excludes any gas that has an absolute vapour
    pressure of not more than 300 kPa at 50°C or that is not completely
    gaseous at 20°C and the standard pressure of 101.3 kPa. ([gaz sous
    pression]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819161 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions --- aerosols

- **[[7.5.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.5.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any product that is classified in a
  category of the hazard class "Aerosols" need not be classified in any
  category of this hazard class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions --- chemicals under pressure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any product that is classified in a category of the
  hazard class "Chemicals Under Pressure" must not be classified in any
  category of this hazard class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A gas under pressure is classified in a category of
  this hazard class in accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="1387077">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Gases Under Pressure —
  Compressed Gas</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">A gas that when packaged under pressure
  is entirely gaseous at -50°C, including all gases with a critical
  temperature ≤ -50°C</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Gases Under Pressure —
  Liquefied Gas</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A gas that when packaged under pressure is
  partially liquid at temperatures &gt; -50°C</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Gases Under Pressure —
  Refrigerated Liquefied Gas</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A gas that when packaged is partially liquid
  because of its low temperature</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">4</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Gases Under Pressure — Dissolved Gas</td>
  <td>A gas that when packaged under pressure is dissolved in a liquid
  phase solvent</td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

::: HistoricalNote

### [SUBPART 6]{.HLabel2}[Flammable Liquids]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819170 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819171 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[7.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.6 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Subpart.

[[appropriate closed-cup method]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[appropriate closed-cup method]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    methods listed in paragraph 2.6.4.2.5 of the GHS, as amended from
    time to time. ([méthode de creuset fermé
    appropriée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[flammable liquid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[flammable liquid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a liquid that has a
    flash point of not more than 93°C. ([liquide
    inflammable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819176 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions --- aerosols

- **[[7.6.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.6.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any product that is classified in a
  category of the hazard class "Aerosols" need not be classified in any
  category of this hazard class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions --- chemicals under pressure

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Any product that is classified in a category of the
  hazard class "Chemicals Under Pressure" must not be classified in any
  category of this hazard class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A flammable liquid is classified in a category of
  this hazard class in accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="819182">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Flammable Liquids —
  Category 1</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">A liquid that has a flash point &lt; 23°C
  and initial boiling point ≤ 35°C</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Flammable Liquids — Category
  2</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid that has a flash point &lt; 23°C and
  initial boiling point &gt; 35°C</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Flammable Liquids — Category
  3</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid that has a flash point ≥ 23°C and ≤
  60°C</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">4</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Flammable Liquids — Category 4</td>
  <td>A liquid that has a flash point &gt; 60°C and ≤ 93°C</td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of flash point ---
  substance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a liquid that is a substance, the
  flash point must be determined by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tests using an appropriate closed-cup method; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use of scientific literature that reports a value
    obtained from an appropriate closed-cup method.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of flash point ---
  mixture

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a liquid that is a mixture, the flash
  point must be determined by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tests using an appropriate closed-cup method; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use of an applicable calculation method under
    conditions for which it has been validated according to generally
    accepted standards of good scientific practice at the time the
    validation was carried out.

::: HistoricalNote

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1386229-1387088.html"
  role="button"}

### [SUBPART 7]{.HLabel2}[Flammable Solids]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819196 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819197 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[7.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.7 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Subpart.

[[flammable solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[flammable solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a readily combustible
    solid or a solid that is liable to cause or contribute to fire
    through friction. ([solide inflammable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[readily combustible solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[readily combustible solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a powdered,
    granular or pasty mixture or substance that can be easily ignited by
    brief contact with an ignition source and, when ignited, has a flame
    that spreads rapidly. ([solide facilement
    inflammable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819202 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions --- aerosols

- **[[7.7.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.7.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any product that is classified in a
  category of the hazard class "Aerosols" need not be classified in any
  category of this hazard class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions --- chemicals under pressure

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Any product that is classified in a category of the
  hazard class "Chemicals Under Pressure" must not be classified in any
  category of this hazard class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A flammable solid that is a readily combustible solid
  is classified in a category of this hazard class, based on results
  from testing performed in accordance with the burning rate test in
  sub-section 33.2 of Part III of the Manual of Tests and Criteria, in
  accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="819208">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Flammable Solids — Category
  1</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top"><p>A solid that is</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> other than a metal powder, in
  respect of which</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> the burning time is &lt; 45 s
  or the burning rate is &gt; 2.2 mm/s, and</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> the wetted zone does not
  stop the fire or stops the fire for less than 4 min; or</p></li>
  </ul></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> a metal powder, in respect of
  which the burning time is ≤ 5 min</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Flammable Solids — Category 2</td>
  <td><p>A solid that is</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> other than a metal powder, in
  respect of which</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> the burning time is &lt; 45 s
  or the burning rate is &gt; 2.2 mm/s, and</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> the wetted zone stops the
  fire for at least 4 min; or</p></li>
  </ul></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> a metal powder, in respect of
  which the burning time is &gt; 5 min and ≤ 10 min</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fire through friction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A flammable solid that is a solid that is liable to
  cause or contribute to fire through friction is classified in the
  category "Flammable Solids --- Category 2".

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-272, s. 27](#1386231-1387101){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1386231-1387101.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [SUBPART 8]{.HLabel2}[Self-reactive Substances and Mixtures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819216 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819217 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[7.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.8 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Subpart.

[[as packaged]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[as packaged]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means packaged in the form and
    condition described in test series B, D, G and H of Part II of the
    Manual of Tests and Criteria. ([tel qu'il est
    emballé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[explosive properties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[explosive properties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the properties of
    a self-reactive substance or mixture that, in laboratory testing
    according to test series A, C or E of Part II of the Manual of Tests
    and Criteria, make the substance or mixture liable to detonate,
    deflagrate rapidly or show a violent effect when heated under
    confinement. ([propriétés explosives]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[self-reactive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[self-reactive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a
    thermally unstable liquid or solid product, mixture or substance,
    liable to undergo a strongly exothermic decomposition, having a heat
    of decomposition equal to or greater than 300 J/g, even without
    participation of oxygen. ([autoréactif]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819223 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions

- **[[7.8.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.8.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following need not be classified
  in any category of this hazard class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} mixtures or substances, or mixtures or substances
    as packaged, that are classified in a category of the hazard class
    "Organic Peroxides";

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} liquid or solid mixtures that are classified in a
    category of the hazard class "Oxidizing Liquids" or "Oxidizing
    Solids", and contain less than 5.0% of combustible organic
    substances; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} liquid or solid substances that are classified in a
    category of the hazard class "Oxidizing Liquids" or "Oxidizing
    Solids".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a self-reactive substance
  or mixture is classified in a category of this hazard class, based on
  results from testing performed in accordance with test series A to H
  of Part II of the Manual of Tests and Criteria, in accordance with the
  following table:

  <figure id="819232">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Self-reactive Substances
  and Mixtures — Type A</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">A liquid or solid that, as packaged, is
  liable to detonate, or deflagrate rapidly</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Self-reactive Substances and
  Mixtures — Type B</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid or solid that possesses explosive
  properties and, as packaged, neither detonates, nor deflagrates rapidly,
  but is liable to undergo a thermal explosion in that package</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Self-reactive Substances and
  Mixtures — Type C</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid or solid that possesses explosive
  properties and, as packaged, neither detonates, nor deflagrates rapidly
  nor undergoes a thermal explosion in that package</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">4</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Self-reactive Substances and
  Mixtures — Type D</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom"><p>In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid
  that</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> detonates partially, does not
  deflagrate rapidly and shows no violent effect when heated under
  confinement;</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> does not detonate,
  deflagrates slowly and shows no violent effect when heated under
  confinement; or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> neither detonates nor
  deflagrates, and shows a medium effect when heated under
  confinement</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">5</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Self-reactive Substances and
  Mixtures — Type E</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid that
  neither detonates nor deflagrates and shows low or no effect when heated
  under confinement</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">6</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Self-reactive Substances and
  Mixtures — Type F</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom"><p>In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid
  that neither detonates in the cavitated state nor deflagrates and</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> shows low or no effect when
  heated under confinement, as well as low or no explosive power;
  or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> shows no effect when heated
  under confinement nor any explosive power, and either</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> has a SADT &lt; 60°C when
  evaluated in a 50 kg package, or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> in the case of a liquid
  mixture, has a diluent that is used for desensitization with a boiling
  point &lt; 150°C</p></li>
  </ul></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">7</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Self-reactive Substances and Mixtures — Type
  G</td>
  <td><p>In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid that neither detonates
  in the cavitated state nor deflagrates, shows no effect when heated
  under confinement nor any explosive power, and either</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> has a SADT of 60°C to 75°C
  when evaluated in a 50 kg package, or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> in the case of a liquid
  mixture, has a diluent that is used for desensitization with a boiling
  point ≥ 150°C</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion after evaluation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A mixture or substance with a self-accelerating
  decomposition temperature greater than 75°C when evaluated in a 50 kg
  package need not be classified in any category of this hazard class.

### [SUBPART 9]{.HLabel2}[Pyrophoric Liquids]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819240 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819241 .Subheading}

Definition of [[pyrophoric liquid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.9 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[pyrophoric liquid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a liquid that is liable
to ignite within five minutes after coming into contact with air.

#### [Classification in the Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819244 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Category

**[[7.9.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.9.1 .sectionLabel}** A pyrophoric liquid
is classified in the category of this hazard class, based on results
from testing performed in accordance with test N.3 of sub-section 33.4.5
of Part III of the Manual of Tests and Criteria, in accordance with the
following table:

<figure id="819247">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom top" scope="col">Category</th>
<th class="borderBottom top" scope="col">Criteria</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight top">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight top">Pyrophoric Liquids — Category 1</td>
<td class="top"><p>A liquid that, within 5 min, either</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> ignites when added to an
inert carrier and after coming into contact with air, or</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> ignites or chars a filter
paper, after coming into contact with air</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

::: HistoricalNote

  role="button"}

### [SUBPART 10]{.HLabel2}[Pyrophoric Solids]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819253 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819254 .Subheading}

Definition of [[pyrophoric solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.10 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[pyrophoric solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a solid that is liable to
ignite within five minutes after coming into contact with air.

#### [Classification in the Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819257 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Category

**[[7.10.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.10.1 .sectionLabel}** A pyrophoric
solid is classified in the category of this hazard class, based on
results from testing performed in accordance with test N.2 of
sub-section 33.4.4 of Part III of the Manual of Tests and Criteria, in
accordance with the following table:

<figure id="819260">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight top">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight top">Pyrophoric Solids — Category 1</td>
<td class="top">A solid that ignites within 5 min after coming into
contact with air</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-272, s. 29](#1386238-1387109){.wb-lbx

### [SUBPART 11]{.HLabel2}[Self-heating Substances and Mixtures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819266 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819267 .Subheading}

Definition of [[self-heating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.11 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[self-heating]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a solid or
liquid, liable to self-heat by reaction with air and without energy
supply.

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819270 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions

- **[[7.11.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.11.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following need not be classified
  in any category of this hazard class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a liquid classified in the category of the hazard
    class "Pyrophoric Liquids"; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a solid classified in the category of the hazard
    class "Pyrophoric Solids".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a self-heating substance
  or mixture is classified in a category of this hazard class, based on
  results from testing performed in accordance with test N.4 of
  sub-section 33.4.6 of Part III of the Manual of Tests and Criteria, in
  accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="1387113">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Self-heating Substances and
  Mixtures — Category 1</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">A solid or liquid in respect of which a
  positive result is obtained in a test using a 25 mm sample cube at
  140°C</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight top">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight top">Self-heating Substances and Mixtures —
  Category 2</td>
  <td class="top"><p>A solid or liquid in respect of which a positive
  result is obtained in a test using a 100 mm sample cube at 140°C, a
  negative result is obtained in a test using a 25 mm sample cube at 140°C
  and</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> the solid or liquid is packed
  in packages with a volume &gt; 3 m³;</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> a positive result is obtained
  in a test using a 100 mm sample cube at 120°C and the solid or liquid is
  packed in packages with a volume &gt; 450 l; or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> a positive result is obtained
  in a test using a 100 mm sample cube at 100°C</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion after evaluation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A mixture or substance with a temperature of
  spontaneous combustion higher than 50°C for a volume of 27 m^3^ need
  not be classified in any category of this hazard class.

::: HistoricalNote

### [SUBPART 12]{.HLabel2}[Substances and Mixtures Which, in Contact with Water, Emit Flammable Gases]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819286 .Subheading}

#### [General Provision]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819287 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[7.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.12 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
substances and mixtures which, in contact with water, emit flammable
gases are liquids and solids that, by interaction with water, are liable
to become spontaneously flammable or give off flammable gases in
dangerous quantities, that is, in quantities that are greater than one
litre of gas per kilogram of the mixture or substance per hour.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-272, s. 31](#1386242-1387125){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1386242-1387125.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7.12-20150211.html "Link to previous version of section 7.12"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819290 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions

- **[[7.12.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.12.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following liquids or solids need
  not be classified in any category of this hazard class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} those that have a chemical structure that does not
    contain metals or metalloids;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} those that have been shown, through accumulated
    experience in production or handling, not to react with water; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} those that are soluble in water to form a stable
    mixture.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A liquid or solid which, in contact with water, emits
  flammable gases is classified in a category of this hazard class,
  based on results from testing performed in accordance with test N.5 of
  sub-section 33.5.4 of Part III of the Manual of Tests and Criteria, in
  accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="819299">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Substances and Mixtures
  Which, in Contact with Water, Emit Flammable Gases — Category 1</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top"><p>A liquid or solid that</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> reacts with water at ambient
  temperature and produces a gas that is liable to ignite
  spontaneously;</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> reacts with water at ambient
  temperature such that the rate of evolution of flammable gas is ≥
  10 l/kg of liquid or solid over any one minute; or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> reacts with water at ambient
  temperature to ignite spontaneously in any step of the test
  procedure</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Substances and Mixtures Which,
  in Contact with Water, Emit Flammable Gases — Category 2</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid or solid that reacts with water at
  ambient temperature such that the maximum rate of evolution of flammable
  gas is ≥ 20 l/kg of liquid or solid per hour</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Substances and Mixtures Which, in Contact with
  Water, Emit Flammable Gases — Category 3</td>
  <td>A liquid or solid that reacts with water at ambient temperature such
  that the maximum rate of evolution of flammable gas is &gt; 1 l/kg of
  liquid or solid per hour</td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

### [SUBPART 13]{.HLabel2}[Oxidizing Liquids]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819305 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819306 .Subheading}

Definition of [[oxidizing liquid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.13 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[oxidizing liquid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a liquid, whether or not
combustible, that is liable to cause or contribute to the combustion of
other material.

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819309 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions

- **[[7.13.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.13.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following liquids need not be
  classified in any category of this hazard class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any organic liquid that does not contain oxygen,
    fluorine or chlorine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any organic liquid that contains oxygen, fluorine
    or chlorine if those elements are chemically bonded only to carbon
    or hydrogen; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any inorganic liquid that does not contain oxygen
    or halogens.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An oxidizing liquid is classified in a category of
  this hazard class, based on results from testing performed in
  accordance with test O.2 of sub-section 34.4.2 of Part III of the
  Manual of Tests and Criteria, in accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="819318">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Oxidizing Liquids —
  Category 1</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">A liquid that, when tested in a 1:1
  mixture, by mass, with cellulose, spontaneously ignites, or exhibits a
  mean pressure rise time &lt; the mean pressure rise time of a 1:1
  mixture, by mass, of 50.0% perchloric acid and cellulose</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Oxidizing Liquids — Category
  2</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid that, when tested in a 1:1 mixture,
  by mass, with cellulose, exhibits a mean pressure rise time ≤ the mean
  pressure rise time of a 1:1 mixture, by mass, of 40.0% aqueous sodium
  chlorate solution and cellulose</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Oxidizing Liquids — Category 3</td>
  <td>A liquid that, when tested in a 1:1 mixture, by mass, with
  cellulose, exhibits a mean pressure rise time ≤ the mean pressure rise
  time of a 1:1 mixture, by mass, of 65.0% aqueous nitric acid and
  cellulose</td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

### [SUBPART 14]{.HLabel2}[Oxidizing Solids]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819324 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819325 .Subheading}

Definition of [[oxidizing solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.14 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[oxidizing solid]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a solid, whether or not
combustible, that is liable to cause or contribute to the combustion of
other material.

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819328 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions

- **[[7.14.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.14.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following solids need not be
  classified in any category of this hazard class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any organic solid that does not contain oxygen,
    fluorine or chlorine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any organic solid that contains oxygen, fluorine or
    chlorine if those elements are chemically bonded only to carbon or
    hydrogen; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any inorganic solid that does not contain oxygen or
    halogens.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An oxidizing solid is classified in a category of
  this hazard class, based on results from testing performed in
  accordance with test O.1 of sub-section 34.4.1 of Part III of the
  Manual of Tests and Criteria or test O.3 of sub-section 34.4.3 of that
  Part, in accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="1387132">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 3</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria Using
  Test O.1</th>
  <th class="borderBottom" scope="col">Criteria Using Test O.3</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="row">Oxidizing
  Solids — Category 1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">A solid that, when tested
  in a 4:1 or 1:1 mixture, by mass, with cellulose, exhibits a mean
  burning time &lt; the mean burning time of a 3:2 mixture, by mass, of
  potassium bromate and cellulose</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A solid that, when tested in a 4:1 or 1:1
  mixture, by mass, with cellulose, exhibits a mean burning rate &gt; the
  mean burning rate of a 3:1 mixture, by mass, of calcium peroxide and
  cellulose</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom" scope="row">Oxidizing Solids —
  Category 2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">A solid that, when tested in a
  4:1 or 1:1 mixture, by mass, with cellulose, exhibits a mean burning
  time ≤ the mean burning time of a 2:3 mixture, by mass, of potassium
  bromate and cellulose</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A solid that, when tested in a 4:1 or 1:1
  mixture, by mass, with cellulose, exhibits a mean burning rate ≥ the
  mean burning rate of a 1:1 mixture, by mass, of calcium peroxide and
  cellulose</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight top">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight top" scope="row">Oxidizing Solids — Category
  3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight top">A solid that, when tested in a 4:1 or 1:1
  mixture, by mass, with cellulose, exhibits a mean burning time ≤ the
  mean burning time of a 3:7 mixture, by mass, of potassium bromate and
  cellulose</td>
  <td>A solid that, when tested in a 4:1 or 1:1 mixture, by mass, with
  cellulose, exhibits a mean burning rate ≥ the mean burning rate of a 1:2
  mixture, by mass, of calcium peroxide and cellulose</td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

::: HistoricalNote

### [SUBPART 15]{.HLabel2}[Organic Peroxides]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819343 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819344 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[7.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.15 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Subpart.

[[as packaged]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[as packaged]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means packaged in the form and
    condition described in test series B, D, G and H of Part II of the
    Manual of Tests and Criteria. ([tel qu'il est
    emballé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[explosive properties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[explosive properties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the properties of
    an organic peroxide that, in laboratory testing according to test
    series A, C or E of Part II of the Manual of Tests and Criteria,
    make the liquid or solid liable to detonate, deflagrate rapidly or
    show a violent effect when heated under confinement. ([propriétés
    explosives]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[organic peroxide]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[organic peroxide]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an organic liquid or
    solid that contains the bivalent -O-O- structure. ([peroxyde
    organique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819350 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusions

- **[[7.15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.15.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An organic peroxide that contains
  any of the following need not be classified in any category of this
  hazard class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not more than 1.0% available oxygen from the
    organic peroxides when containing not more than 1.0% hydrogen
    peroxide; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} not more than 0.5% available oxygen from the
    organic peroxides when containing more than 1.0% but not more than
    7.0% hydrogen peroxide.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Available oxygen content

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The available oxygen content, in percent, of an
  organic peroxide mixture referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (b) is
  determined by the following formula:

  ::: {#819358 .Subsection}
  <figure>
  <div class="imageGroupcenter">
  <img src="/images/SOR-2015-17img/SOR-2015-17_ef001.jpg"
  alt="The available oxygen content, in percent, of an organic peroxide mixture is equal to 16 times the summation, represented by the capital sigma symbol, of the series running from i to n of, opening parenthesis, the product of n, subscript i, and c, subscript i, divided by m, subscript i, closing parenthesis." />
  </div>
  </figure>

  where

  [n~i~]{.dfn}
  :   is the number of peroxygen groups per molecule of organic peroxide
      i;

  [c~i~]{.dfn}
  :   is the concentration (mass %) of organic peroxide i; and

  [m~i~]{.dfn}
  :   is the molecular mass of organic peroxide i.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Categories

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An organic peroxide is classified in a category of
  this hazard class, based on results from testing performed in
  accordance with test series A to H of Part II of the Manual of Tests
  and Criteria, in accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="819366">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom top" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom top" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Organic Peroxides —
  Type A</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">A liquid or solid that, as packaged, is
  liable to detonate, or deflagrate rapidly</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Organic Peroxides — Type B</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid or solid that possesses explosive
  properties and, as packaged, neither detonates, nor deflagrates rapidly,
  but is liable to undergo a thermal explosion in that package</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Organic Peroxides — Type C</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A liquid or solid that possesses explosive
  properties and, as packaged, neither detonates, nor deflagrates rapidly,
  nor undergoes a thermal explosion in that package</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">4</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Organic Peroxides — Type D</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom"><p>In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid
  that</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> detonates partially, but does
  not deflagrate rapidly and shows no violent effect when heated under
  confinement;</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> does not detonate, but
  deflagrates slowly and shows no violent effect when heated under
  confinement; or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> neither detonates nor
  deflagrates, but shows a medium effect when heated under
  confinement</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">5</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Organic Peroxides — Type E</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid that
  neither detonates nor deflagrates, and shows low or no effect when
  heated under confinement</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">6</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Organic Peroxides — Type F</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom"><p>In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid
  that neither detonates in the cavitated state nor deflagrates and</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> shows low or no effect when
  heated under confinement, as well as low or no explosive power;
  or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> shows no effect when heated
  under confinement nor any explosive power, and either</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> has a SADT &lt; 60°C when
  evaluated in a 50 kg package, or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> in the case of a liquid
  mixture, has a diluent that is used for desensitization with a boiling
  point &lt; 150°C</p></li>
  </ul></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">7</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Organic Peroxides — Type G</td>
  <td><p>In laboratory testing, a liquid or solid that neither detonates
  in the cavitated state nor deflagrates, shows no effect when heated
  under confinement nor any explosive power, and either</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> has a SADT ≥ 60°C when
  evaluated in a 50 kg package, or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> in the case of a liquid
  mixture, has a diluent that is used for desensitization with a boiling
  point ≥ 150°C</p></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mixtures --- organic peroxides

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), a mixture of organic
  peroxides must be classified in the same category as the most
  hazardous organic peroxide in the mixture, unless data of the types
  referred to in subparagraph 2.1(a)(i) or (ii) or (b)(i) or (ii) are
  available for the mixture as a whole and the data support the
  conclusion that the mixture must be classified in a category that
  represents a less severe hazard.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mixtures --- Type G organic peroxides

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A mixture of two or more Type G organic peroxides
  must be classified in the category "Organic Peroxides --- Type G",
  unless the self-accelerating decomposition temperature of the mixture
  results in the mixture being classified in a category that represents
  a more severe hazard.

::: HistoricalNote

### [SUBPART 16]{.HLabel2}[Corrosive to Metals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819374 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819375 .Subheading}

Definition of [[corrosive to metals]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.16 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[corrosive to metals]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a
mixture or substance, liable to damage or destroy metal by chemical
action.

#### [Classification in the Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819378 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Category

**[[7.16.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.16.1 .sectionLabel}** A mixture or
substance that is corrosive to metals is classified in the category of
this hazard class, based on results from testing performed in accordance
with sub-section 37.4 of Part III of the Manual of Tests and Criteria,
in accordance with the following table:

<figure id="819381">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight top">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight top">Corrosive to Metals — Category 1</td>
<td class="top">A mixture or substance that has a corrosion rate on
either steel or aluminium surfaces that is &gt; 6.25 mm per year at a
test temperature of 55°C</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

### [SUBPART 17]{.HLabel2}[Combustible Dusts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819387 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819388 .Subheading}

Definition of [[combustible dust]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.17 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[combustible dust]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a mixture or substance
that is in the form of finely divided solid particles that, upon
ignition, is liable to catch fire or explode when dispersed in air.

#### [Classification in the Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819391 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Category

**[[7.17.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.17.1 .sectionLabel}** A combustible
dust is classified in the category of this hazard class in accordance
with the following table:

<figure id="819394">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight top">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight top">Combustible Dusts — Category 1</td>
<td class="top"><p>A mixture or substance that</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> has been shown to, upon
ignition, catch fire or explode when dispersed in air; or</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> is classified in a category
of the hazard class “Flammable Solids” and 5.0% or more of its
composition by weight is a flammable solid and has a particle size ≤
500 µm</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

### [SUBPART 18]{.HLabel2}[Simple Asphyxiants]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819400 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819401 .Subheading}

Definition of [[simple asphyxiant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.18 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[simple asphyxiant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any gas that is liable
to cause asphyxiation by the displacement of air.

#### [Classification in the Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819404 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Category

**[[7.18.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.18.1 .sectionLabel}** A simple
asphyxiant is classified in the category of this hazard class in
accordance with the following table:

### [SUBPART 20]{.HLabel2}[Physical Hazards Not Otherwise Classified]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819426 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819427 .Subheading}

Definition of [[physical hazard not otherwise
classified]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[7.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.20 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[physical hazard not otherwise classified]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
physical hazard presented by a product, mixture, material or substance
that is different from any other physical hazard addressed by any other
Subpart in this Part and that has the characteristic of occurring by
chemical reaction and resulting in the serious injury or death of a
person at the time the reaction occurs.

#### [Classification in the Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819430 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Category

**[[7.20.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.20.1 .sectionLabel}** A product,
mixture, material or substance is classified in the category of this
hazard class in accordance with the following table:

class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">

<th class="borderBottom top" scope="col">Criteria</th>
</tr>

<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight top">1</td>

— Category 1</td>

a physical hazard not otherwise classified</td>

### [SUBPART 21]{.HLabel2}[Chemicals Under Pressure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1387162 .Subheading}

#### [Definition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1387163 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[Chemicals Under
Pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[7.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.21 .sectionLabel}** In this Subpart,
[[chemicals under pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means liquids or solids
that are packaged in a receptacle --- other than an aerosol dispenser
--- and that are pressurized with a gas at a gauge pressure of 200 kPa
or more at 20°C but excludes any [[gas under
pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in section 7.5.

  category of this hazard class.

## [PART 8]{.HLabel1}[Health Hazard Classes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-819439 .Part}

### [SUBPART 1]{.HLabel2}[Acute Toxicity]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819440 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819441 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[8.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Subpart.

[[acute toxicant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[acute toxicant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a mixture or substance
    that is liable to cause acute toxicity, or a mixture or substance
    that, upon contact with water, releases a gaseous substance that is
    liable to cause acute toxicity. ([toxique aigu]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[acute toxicity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[acute toxicity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} refers to serious adverse
    health effects, including death, occurring following

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} oral or dermal administration of a single dose of
      a mixture or substance, or multiple doses given within 24 hours;
      or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an inhalation exposure to a mixture or substance
      of four hours or of a duration that is converted to four hours in
      accordance with subsection 8.1.1(4). ([toxicité
      aiguë]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dust]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dust]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means solid particles that are
    suspended in a gas, usually air. ([poussières]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[mist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[mist]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means liquid droplets that are
    suspended in the air. ([brouillard]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-272, s. 38](#1386261-1387201){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1386261-1387201.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.1-20150211.html "Link to previous version of section 8.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Classification in a Category of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819450 .Subheading}

##### [Classification of Substances]{.HTitleText4} {#h-819451 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}LD~50~ or LC~50~ --- associated range

- **[[8.1.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An acute toxicant that is a
  substance is classified, with respect to each applicable route of
  exposure, in a category of this hazard class in accordance with the
  tables to subsection (3) if it has an LD~50~ by the oral or dermal
  exposure route, or an LC~50~ by the inhalation exposure route, that
  falls into one of the ranges indicated in the applicable table to that
  subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contact with water --- gaseous
  substance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an acute toxicant that is a substance is not
  classified in accordance with subsection (1) with respect to the
  inhalation route of exposure in a category of this hazard class in
  accordance with Table 3 to subsection (3) and, upon contact with
  water, the acute toxicant releases a gaseous substance that has an
  LC~50~ that falls into one of the ranges indicated in that table, it
  is classified in accordance with that table with regard to that range.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}LD~50~ or LC~50~ not available

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an LD~50~ by the oral or dermal exposure route or
  an LC~50~ by the inhalation exposure route is not available, an acute
  toxicity point estimate must be established in accordance with the
  table to section 8.1.7, and the acute toxicant must be classified
  based on that acute toxicity point estimate, with respect to each
  applicable route of exposure, in a category of this hazard class in
  accordance with the following tables:

  <figure id="819459">

  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">

  </tr>

  Category 4</td>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One-hour exposure period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of Table 3 to subsection (3), the
  LC~50~ is based on a four-hour exposure period. If existing acute
  inhalation toxicity data have been generated according to a one-hour
  exposure period, the LC~50~ for gases and vapours must be divided by
  two, and the LC~50~ for dusts and mists must be divided by four.

::: HistoricalNote

  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.1.1-20150211.html "Link to previous version of section 8.1.1"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Classification of Mixtures]{.HTitleText4} {#h-819482 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of provisions

- **[[8.1.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The classification of a mixture as
  an acute toxicant in a category of this hazard class must proceed in
  accordance with the order of sections 8.1.3 to 8.1.6.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concentrations for the purpose of
  classification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Only ingredients present at concentrations equal to
  or greater than the concentration limit of 1.0% --- w/w for solids,
  liquids, dusts, mists and vapours and v/v for gases --- must be
  considered for the purpose of classification.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Data available for mixture as a whole

**[[8.1.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1.3 .sectionLabel}** If data of the
types referred to in subparagraphs 2.1(a)(i) to (iv) are available for
the mixture as a whole, the mixture must be classified as an acute
toxicant in accordance with section 8.1.1.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Data available for use of bridging
principles

**[[8.1.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1.4 .sectionLabel}** If data are
available to enable the characterization of the mixture as an acute
toxicant, in accordance with the bridging principles referred to in
subsections 2.3(3) to (8), the mixture must be classified in a category
of this hazard class in accordance with those subsections.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Data available for all ingredients

**[[8.1.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1.5 .sectionLabel}** If data are
available for all ingredients in the mixture, the mixture must be
classified as an acute toxicant in accordance with section 8.1.1 using
the ATE of the mixture that is determined in respect of each applicable
route of exposure by the following formula:

::: {#819494 .Section}
<figure>
<div class="imageGroupcenter">
<img src="/images/SOR-2015-17img/SOR-2015-17_e002.jpg"
alt="ATE subscript mix is equal to 100 divided by, opening square bracket, the summation, represented by the capital sigma symbol, from n, of the series capital C, subscript i, divided by ATE, subscript i, closing square bracket." />
</div>
</figure>

where

[ATE~mix~]{.dfn}
:   is the ATE of the mixture determined using this formula;

[C~i~]{.dfn}
:   is the concentration of ingredient i;

[n]{.dfn}
:   is the number of ingredients and i is running from 1 to n;

[ATE~i~]{.dfn}
:   is the ATE of ingredient i, which is either
    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the LD~50~ or the LC~50~ based on or converted to
      a four-hour exposure period, for i, or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the LD~50~ or the LC~50~ is unavailable, the
      acute toxicity point estimate established for i in accordance with
      the table to section 8.1.7; and

[i]{.dfn}
:   is each ingredient in the mixture with
    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an ATE within the ranges set out in the
      applicable table to subsection 8.1.1(3),

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an oral or dermal LD~50~ greater than 2000 mg/kg
      body weight but less than or equal to 5000 mg/kg body weight, or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an LC~50~ based on or converted to a four-hour
      exposure period within a range having an amplitude comparable to
      the one in paragraph (b).
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Data not available for all ingredients

**[[8.1.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1.6 .sectionLabel}** If the ATE is not
available for one or more ingredients of the mixture, the mixture must
be classified as an acute toxicant in accordance with section 8.1.1
using the ATE of the mixture that is determined in respect of each
applicable route of exposure according to the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if data permit the ATE to be estimated for each of
  those ingredients in accordance with established scientific
  principles, the formula in section 8.1.5 must be used;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if data do not permit the ATE to be estimated for an
  ingredient in accordance with established scientific principles, and
  the concentration of the ingredient in the mixture is equal to or
  greater than the concentration limit of 1.0%, the mixture is
  classified based only on the ingredients having an ATE, such that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the total concentration of all ingredients with
    unknown acute toxicity is less than or equal to 10.0% of the
    mixture, the formula in section 8.1.5 must be used, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the total concentration of all ingredients with
    unknown acute toxicity is greater than 10.0% of the mixture, the
    following formula must be used:

    ::: {#819513 .Subparagraph}
    <figure>
    <div class="imageGroupcenter">
    <img src="/images/SOR-2015-17img/SOR-2015-17_e003.jpg"
    alt="ATE subscript mix is equal to the quotient of the numerator of 100 minus, opening parenthesis, the summation, represented by the capital sigma symbol, of the series capital C, subscript unknown, if the value of C is greater than 10.0%, closing parenthesis, divided by the following denominator: opening square bracket, the summation, represented by the symbol capital sigma, from n, of the series capital C, subscript i, divided by ATE, subscript i, closing square bracket." />
    </div>
    </figure>

    where

    [ATE~mix~]{.dfn}
    :   is the ATE of the mixture determined using this formula,

    [C~i~]{.dfn}
    :   is the concentration of ingredient i,

    [C~unknown~]{.dfn}
    :   is the concentration of ingredients i with unknown ATE values,

    [n]{.dfn}
    :   is the number of ingredients and i is running from 1 to n,

    [ATE~i~]{.dfn}
    :   is the ATE of ingredient i, which is either
        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the LD~50~ or the LC~50~ based on or
          converted to a four-hour exposure period, for i, or

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the LD~50~ or the LC~50~ is unavailable,
          the acute toxicity point estimate established for i in
          accordance with the table to section 8.1.7, and

    [i]{.dfn}
    :   is each ingredient in the mixture with
        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an ATE within the ranges set out in the
          applicable table to subsection 8.1.1(3),

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an oral or dermal LD~50~ greater than 2000
          mg/kg body weight but less than or equal to 5000 mg/kg body
          weight, or

        - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an LC~50~ based on or converted to a
          four-hour exposure period within a range having an amplitude
          comparable to the one in paragraph (b).
    :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conversion from range to point estimate

- **[[8.1.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a formula in section 8.1.5 or
  8.1.6 is used, an acute toxicity point estimate must be determined, in
  accordance with the table to subsection (2), for each ingredient for
  which only that ingredient's classification category or experimentally
  obtained acute toxicity range is available.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}More than one range

  for an ingredient does not fall entirely within any of the ranges set
  out in column 2 of the following table, the converted acute toxicity
  point estimate for that ingredient for the purposes of column 3 is the
  lowest value of the experimentally obtained acute toxicity range.

### [SUBPART 2]{.HLabel2}[Skin Corrosion/Irritation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-819535 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819536 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[8.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.2 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Subpart.

[[skin corrosion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[skin corrosion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the production of
    irreversible damage to the skin, namely, visible necrosis through
    the epidermis and into the dermis, occurring after exposure to a
    mixture or substance, and includes ulcers, bleeding, bloody scabs
    and, within a 14-day observation period, discoloration due to
    blanching of the skin, complete areas of alopecia, and
    scars. ([corrosion cutanée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[skin-corrosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[skin-corrosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a
    mixture or substance, liable to cause skin corrosion. ([corrosif
    pour la peau]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[skin-irritant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[skin-irritant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a
    mixture or substance, liable to cause skin irritation. ([irritant
    pour la peau]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[skin irritation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[skin irritation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the production of
    reversible damage to the skin occurring after exposure to a mixture
    or substance. ([irritation cutanée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote

#### [Classification in a Category or Subcategory of the Class]{.HTitleText3} {#h-819543 .Subheading}

##### [Classification of Substances]{.HTitleText4} {#h-819544 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of provisions

**[[8.2.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.2.1 .sectionLabel}** The classification
of a skin-corrosive substance or a skin-irritant substance in a category
or subcategory of this hazard class must proceed in accordance with the
order of sections 8.2.2 to 8.2.7, unless, after applying subsections
8.2.2(1) to (3), the substance is not classified further to subsection
8.2.2(4).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Human data --- skin corrosion

- **[[8.2.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.2.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A substance for which human data
  demonstrate that it is a skin-corrosive substance is classified in the
  category "Skin Corrosion --- Category 1".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Animal data --- skin corrosion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A substance for which purposely generated animal data
  demonstrate that it is a skin-corrosive substance is classified in the
  category "Skin Corrosion --- Category 1" and is, if the applicable
  data are available, further classified in accordance with the
  following table:

  <figure id="819552">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
  scope="col">Subcategory</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top">Skin Corrosion — Category
  1A</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom top">A substance that, according to animal
  data acquired from a scientifically validated method, produces
  irreversible damage to the skin after an exposure of three minutes or
  less, and within one hour of observation, in at least one animal</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Skin Corrosion — Category
  1B</td>
  <td class="noBorderBottom">A substance that, according to animal data
  acquired from a scientifically validated method, produces irreversible
  damage to the skin after an exposure of more than three minutes and up
  to and including one hour, and within 14 days of observation, in at
  least one animal</td>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">Skin Corrosion — Category 1C</td>
  <td>A substance that, according to animal data acquired from a
  scientifically validated method, produces irreversible damage to the
  skin after an exposure of more than one hour and up to and including
  four hours, and within 14 days of observation, in at least one
  animal</td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Human or animal data --- skin
  irritation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A substance for which there are human data or
  purposely generated animal data with respect to skin irritation is
  classified in the category "Skin Irritation --- Category 2" in
  accordance with the following table:

  <figure id="819560">
  <table
  class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
  <thead>
  <tr class="topdouble">
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom"></th>
  <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
  <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
  </tr>
  <tr>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Item</th>
  <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Category</th>
  <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Criteria</th>
  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight top">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight top">Skin Irritation — Category 2</td>
  <td class="top"><p>A substance</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> that, according to human
  data, is skin-irritant; or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> in respect of which animal
  data reveal</p>
  <ul>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> in the case of data acquired
  from a test performed in accordance with the OECD Guideline for the
  Testing of Chemicals, No. 404, entitled Acute Dermal
  Irritation/Corrosion, as amended from time to time, a mean score of ≥
  2.3 and ≤ 4.0 for erythema and eschar or for edema in at least two of
  three animals from gradings at 24, 48 and 72 hours after patch removal
  or, if reactions are delayed, from gradings on three consecutive days
  after the onset of skin reactions,</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> in the case of data acquired
  from a scientifically validated method, inflammation, namely, local
  alopecia, hyperkeratosis, hyperplasia and scaling, that persists to the
  end of the observation period specified by the method in at least two
  animals, or</p></li>
  <li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iii)</span> in the case of data
  acquired from a scientifically validated method, evidence of severe skin
  irritation in only one animal</p></li>
  </ul></li>
  </ul></td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No classification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A substance that meets the following conditions need
  not be classified in any category or subcategory of this hazard class:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there are human data or purposely generated animal
    data on the substance, acquired from a scientifically validated
    method, with respect to skin corrosion or skin irritation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the substance is not classified further to
    subsection (1), (2) or (3); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the data referred to in paragraph (a) demonstrate
    that it is neither a skin-corrosive substance nor a skin-irritant
    substance.

:::
